030367:  * Keep structural provenance (program/day/week) locked while allowing the performed evidence itself
030368:  * to be corrected. Every save rebuilds the summary mirror from the edited raw sets. */
030369: function summarizeHistoryLoggedSets(sets, ex, unit) {
030370:     const work = (sets || []).filter(st => st && !st.sub && Number(st.r) > 0);
030371:     if (!work.length)
030372:         return null;
030373:     const numW = st => Number.isFinite(Number(st.w)) ? Number(st.w) : 0;
030374:     const hasPos = work.some(st => numW(st) > 0);
030375:     const pool = hasPos ? work.filter(st => numW(st) > 0) : work;
030376:     if (!pool.length)
030377:         return null;
030378:     const weights = pool.map(numW);
030379:     const maxW = Math.max(...weights);
030380:     if (weights.every(w => w === weights[0])) {
030381:         const atTop = pool.filter(st => numW(st) === maxW);
030382:         return { weight: maxW, reps: Math.min(...atTop.map(st => Math.max(1, Math.round(Number(st.r) || 1)))) };
030383:     }
030384:     const step = ex ? loadStep(ex, unit) : .5;
030385:     const avgW = roundTo(weights.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / weights.length, step || .5);
030386:     const reps = Math.round(pool.reduce((a, st) => a + Math.max(1, Math.round(Number(st.r) || 1)), 0) / pool.length);
030387:     return { weight: avgW, reps };
030388: }
030389: function normalizeEditedHistoryEntry(original, draft) {
030390:     if (!original || !draft)
030391:         return original;
030392:     const out = { ...original };
030393:     const date = Number(draft.date);
030394:     if (Number.isFinite(date) && date > 0)
030395:         out.date = date;
030396:     const duration = Number(draft.durationMin);
030397:     if (Number.isFinite(duration) && duration >= 0)
030398:         out.durationMin = Math.round(duration);
030399:     const perf = {};
030400:     for (const [exId, rawPerf] of Object.entries(draft.perf || {})) {
030401:         const prior = original.perf?.[exId] || {};
030402:         const sourceSets = Array.isArray(rawPerf?.sets) && rawPerf.sets.length
030403:             ? rawPerf.sets
030404:             : (rawPerf?.reps != null ? [{ w: rawPerf.weight ?? 0, r: rawPerf.reps }] : []);
030405:         const sets = [];
030406:         for (const raw of sourceSets) {
030407:             if (!raw)
030408:                 continue;
030409:             const reps = Math.round(Number(raw.r));
030410:             if (!Number.isFinite(reps) || reps <= 0)
030411:                 continue;
030412:             const w0 = raw.w === "" || raw.w == null ? 0 : Number(raw.w);
030413:             if (!Number.isFinite(w0))
030414:                 continue;
030415:             const st = { ...raw, w: w0, r: reps };
030416:             if (raw.rir === "" || raw.rir == null || !Number.isFinite(Number(raw.rir)))
030417:                 delete st.rir;
030418:             else
030419:                 st.rir = Math.max(0, Math.min(10, Number(raw.rir)));
030420:             sets.push(st);

034178:     /* Every write to a syncable list goes through here, so `updatedAt` and tombstones are recorded in ONE
034179:      * place rather than at each of the ~40 call sites that mutate these lists — where the next one added
034180:      * would silently forget to, and a forgotten stamp is a record that loses every merge it ever enters.
034181:      *
034182:      * Detecting what changed is free because the app already updates immutably: a record that came out of
034183:      * the updater with the same object identity it went in with was not touched, and anything else is new
034184:      * or edited and gets stamped. Ids present before and gone after are deletions, and get a tombstone.
034185:      * Undo works without special handling — it re-adds the record, which stamps it with a time later than
034186:      * its own tombstone, and a tombstone only bites when it is newer than the record it points at.
034187:      */
034188:     const stampedSetter = (setRaw) => (updater) => setRaw(prev => {
034189:         const next = typeof updater === "function" ? updater(prev) : updater;
034190:         if (!Array.isArray(next) || !Array.isArray(prev))
034191:             return next;
034192:         const now = Date.now();
034193:         const before = new Map(prev.map(r => [r && r.id, r]));
034194:         const gone = new Set(prev.map(r => r && r.id));
034195:         next.forEach(r => { if (r)
034196:             gone.delete(r.id); });
034197:         if (gone.size)
034198:             setTombs(t => { const out = { ...t }; gone.forEach(id => { if (id != null)
034199:                 out[id] = now; }); return out; });
034200:         return next.map(r => (r && r.id != null && before.get(r.id) !== r) ? { ...r, updatedAt: now } : r);
034201:     });

017993:  *                Union is not a compromise here — it is the correct answer.
017994:  *   saved/cycles/custom
017995:  *                union by id, newest `updatedAt` wins. These are mutable documents, so a genuine
017996:  *                conflict is possible; last-write-wins is the honest, predictable resolution, and the
017997:  *                loser is not silently discarded — it is counted and reported to the caller.
017998:  *   bwLog        union by date (one weigh-in per timestamp).
017999:  *   measurements per-date union, and within a date, per-field union.
018000:  *   perf/exNotes/exSetup/goals
018001:  *                per-key: a key present on one side is kept; a key on both sides resolves to the blob
018002:  *                with the newer savedAt. No per-key clocks exist, so this is the finest granularity
018003:  *                that is actually honest.
018004:  *   banned       union. Un-banning is rare and re-banning is one tap; resurrecting a banned exercise
018005:  *                is a nuisance, whereas dropping a ban silently puts a lift you cannot do back in your
018006:  *                program. Fail toward the safe side.
018007:  *   settings     scalars come from whichever blob was saved more recently, as a group.
018008:  *   tombs        union, newest timestamp wins. A tombstone kills a record only when it is NEWER than
018009:  *                that record's updatedAt — so deleting on one device and then editing on another
018010:  *                resurrects it deliberately, which is what the edit meant.
018011:  *
018012:  * Pure, total, and order-independent: merge(a,b) and merge(b,a) agree, merging twice changes nothing.
018013:  * Property-tested to that effect, because a merge bug is not a visual glitch — it is someone's year.
018014:  */
018015: function mergeStores(local, incoming) {
018016:     if (!incoming || typeof incoming !== "object")
018017:         return { data: local, stats: null };
018018:     if (!local || typeof local !== "object")
018019:         return { data: incoming, stats: null };
018020:     const A = migrateStore(local), B = migrateStore(incoming);
018021:     const aNewer = (A.savedAt || 0) >= (B.savedAt || 0);
018022:     const older = aNewer ? B : A, newer = aNewer ? A : B;
018023:     const tombs = { ...(A.tombs || {}) };
018024:     Object.entries(B.tombs || {}).forEach(([id, t]) => { if (!(tombs[id] >= t))
018025:         tombs[id] = t; });
018026:     const stats = { history: 0, programs: 0, cycles: 0, custom: 0, bw: 0, conflicts: 0 };
018027:     const byId = (a = [], b = [], count) => {
018028:         const m = new Map();
018029:         [...(Array.isArray(a) ? a : []), ...(Array.isArray(b) ? b : [])].forEach(r => {
018030:             if (!r || r.id == null)
018031:                 return;
018032:             const prev = m.get(r.id);
018033:             if (!prev) {
018034:                 m.set(r.id, r);
018035:                 return;
018036:             }
018037:             if (prev === r)
018038:                 return;
018039:             const pu = prev.updatedAt || 0, ru = r.updatedAt || 0;
018040:             if (ru > pu) {
018041:                 m.set(r.id, r);
018042:                 stats.conflicts++;
018043:             }
018044:             else if (ru < pu)
018045:                 stats.conflicts++;
018046:         });

029978: // Weekly-average bodyweight trend. Single weigh-ins are noisy — water, sodium, digestion, and sleep
029979: // can swing the number a kg or more day to day, so comparing "today" against "the first-ever reading"
029980: // (as a raw before/after delta does) can easily read as a stall or a spike that isn't real. Bucketing
029981: // into rolling 7-day windows and comparing WEEKLY AVERAGES smooths that noise into an actual trend.
029982: // Buckets count backward from the most recent entry (not the calendar week), so it works the same
029983: // regardless of which day of the week someone happens to be looking at their trend on, and it's a
029984: // pure function of the log itself rather than depending on the wall-clock "now".
029985: // Returns null when there isn't enough spread to say anything (all entries inside one 7-day window).
029986: /* Every weigh-in records the unit it was taken in, exactly as history sessions do — but until now
029987:    nothing READ that tag. Switching units in Settings converts the whole log, so a single device
029988:    stayed consistent; a MERGE does not, because mergeStores unions two devices' logs by timestamp and
029989:    a phone on lb next to a tablet on kg produces one log holding both. Averaging those raw numbers
029990:    turned a steady 180 lb into a "loss" of 98 units a week. Normalising on read is the fix, and it
029991:    also covers entries written before the tag existed, which are read in the store's own unit. */

029996:         .sort((a, b) => a.date - b.date);
029997: }

018127: // Carry a loaded/imported blob forward to STORE_VERSION. Un-versioned blobs predate versioning and are
018128: // treated as v1. Returns the input untouched for a fresh install (null) or non-object junk so the
018129: // caller's existing validity checks still apply.
018130: function migrateStore(raw) {
018131:     if (!raw || typeof raw !== "object")
018132:         return raw;
018133:     let d = raw;
018134:     let v = typeof d.v === "number" ? d.v : 1;
018135:     while (v < STORE_VERSION) {
018136:         v += 1;
018137:         const fn = STORE_MIGRATIONS[v];
018138:         if (fn)
018139:             d = fn(d);
018140:     }
018141:     return d.v === STORE_VERSION ? d : { ...d, v: STORE_VERSION };
018142: }

035094:      *
035095:      * merge (default) — fold the file into what is already here. This is what makes a backup file work as
035096:      *   a transport between two devices: export on the phone, import on the tablet, and both ends end up
035097:      *   holding the union. Import used to REPLACE, which meant restoring a stale backup onto a phone you
035098:      *   had kept training on silently destroyed everything logged since. See mergeStores.
035099:      *
035100:      * replace — the escape hatch, and the reason Undo can exist: it applies a blob verbatim. The only
035101:      *   caller is the undo path, which hands back the snapshot of the store taken immediately before the
035102:      *   merge. A merge is not a destructive operation, but it IS an irreversible one without this — you
035103:      *   cannot un-union two sets — and a user restoring the wrong file deserves a way back.

017788: /* Body measurements are lengths, but they were being stamped with the WEIGHT unit — an entry read
017789:    `{v: 34, unit: "lb"}` for a 34-inch waist. The display then derived inches-or-centimetres from
017790:    the current weight unit, so switching lb→kg relabelled every past measurement as centimetres
017791:    without converting it: a 34" waist silently became "34 cm". Lengths get their own unit, tagged at
017792:    write time and honoured on read, and switchUnit converts them like every other stored number.

017796: const toLength = (v, from, to) => (from === to ? v : to === "cm" ? v * 2.54 : v / 2.54);

033883: function recordReleaseDiag(code, detail = {}) {
033884:     try {
033885:         const clean = {};
033886:         Object.entries(detail && typeof detail === "object" ? detail : {}).forEach(([k, v]) => {
033887:             if (!RELEASE_DIAG_FIELDS.has(k))
033888:                 return;
033889:             if (!["string", "number", "boolean"].includes(typeof v))
033890:                 return;
033891:             clean[k] = typeof v === "string" ? v.slice(0, 180) : v;
033892:         });
033893:         const rows = readReleaseDiagnostics();
033894:         rows.push({ at: Date.now(), event: String(code || "unknown").slice(0, 80), ...clean });
033895:         localStorage.setItem(RELEASE_DIAG_KEY, JSON.stringify(rows.slice(-RELEASE_DIAG_CAP)));

033899:         return false;

033864: /* ============================== RELEASE DIAGNOSTICS ==============================
033865:  * A tiny bounded event journal for beta/release failures. This is deliberately NOT telemetry: it
033866:  * never leaves the device automatically and it never stores workout history, user notes, names,

033883: function recordReleaseDiag(code, detail = {}) {
033884:     try {
033885:         const clean = {};
033886:         Object.entries(detail && typeof detail === "object" ? detail : {}).forEach(([k, v]) => {
033887:             if (!RELEASE_DIAG_FIELDS.has(k))

033890:                 return;

000233: // inside a Capacitor iOS/Android shell, window.Capacitor is present and isNativePlatform() is true.
000234: // Browser-only behaviors (web Notification permission prompts, PWA-install affordances) check this

000237: function isNativeApp() {
000238:     try {

000240:         if (!cap)
000241:             return false;

014369: /* Memoized on the history array's identity. The session screen asks for a suggestion per exercise
014370:  * (banner + prefill), autoStyleFor consults recovery inside each one, and suggestWeight's auto
014371:  * annotation consults it again — a dozen calls per render, every one sorting and scanning the entire
014372:  * training history to produce the identical answer. Measured at ~8ms per pass on a 200-session
014373:  * history, i.e. half a frame budget on a fast machine before the UI draws anything. History is only
014374:  * ever replaced immutably (every setHistory builds a new array), so reference identity is a sound
014375:  * cache key: same array, same answer. The WeakMap lets an old history be collected the moment nothing
014376:  * else holds it. Time-sensitivity note: readiness decays with wall-clock time, so entries expire
014377:  * after 60s rather than living as long as the array does — recovery percentages drift by the minute,
014378:  * not the millisecond, and a stale-by-a-minute number is indistinguishable in the UI.
014379:  */

014381: function muscleRecovery(history) {
014382:     if (history && typeof history === "object") {
014383:         const hit = __recoveryMemo.get(history);
014384:         if (hit && Date.now() - hit.at < 60000)
014385:             return hit.v;
014386:     }
014387:     const v = muscleRecoveryUncached(history);
014388:     if (history && typeof history === "object")
014389:         __recoveryMemo.set(history, { v, at: Date.now() });
014390:     return v;
014391: }
014392: // --- learned recovery ------------------------------------------------------------------------
014393: // The clock model (30 + sets·2.3h) is a population average; actual recovery is individual and muscle-
014394: // specific. This MEASURES it from the lifter's own history: performance-on-repeat. Each time a muscle
014395: // was retrained, pair the rest gap (hours since it was last trained) with the performance ratio (this
014396: // session's best e1RM ÷ the previous session's). Retrain before you've recovered and performance dips;
014397: // once recovered it holds — so a least-squares line ratio = a + b·gap identifies the rest at which
014398: // performance returns to the prior level, i.e. the recovery time. Shrunk toward the clock model by
014399: // confidence, clamped to a sane [18,120]h band, memoized on (history,part).
014400: //
014401: // Deliberately one-directional and conservative: it will RAISE recovery time for a lifter who
014402: // demonstrably dips on short rest (b>0, intercept<1), which is the case that matters — it protects a
014403: // slow-recovering muscle from being retrained (or trimmed) too eagerly. It stays silent (returns null →
014404: // clock model) when rest doesn't measurably help (b≈0), because "always fine at every gap I've tried"

014368: const BASE_SET_COST = 0.5; // recovery cost of one working set performed at the prescribed reserve
014369: /* Memoized on the history array's identity. The session screen asks for a suggestion per exercise
014370:  * (banner + prefill), autoStyleFor consults recovery inside each one, and suggestWeight's auto
014371:  * annotation consults it again — a dozen calls per render, every one sorting and scanning the entire
014372:  * training history to produce the identical answer. Measured at ~8ms per pass on a 200-session
014373:  * history, i.e. half a frame budget on a fast machine before the UI draws anything. History is only
014374:  * ever replaced immutably (every setHistory builds a new array), so reference identity is a sound
014375:  * cache key: same array, same answer. The WeakMap lets an old history be collected the moment nothing
014376:  * else holds it. Time-sensitivity note: readiness decays with wall-clock time, so entries expire
014377:  * after 60s rather than living as long as the array does — recovery percentages drift by the minute,
014378:  * not the millisecond, and a stale-by-a-minute number is indistinguishable in the UI.
014379:  */
014380: const __recoveryMemo = new WeakMap();
014381: function muscleRecovery(history) {
014382:     if (history && typeof history === "object") {
014383:         const hit = __recoveryMemo.get(history);
014384:         if (hit && Date.now() - hit.at < 60000)
014385:             return hit.v;
014386:     }
014387:     const v = muscleRecoveryUncached(history);
014388:     if (history && typeof history === "object")
014389:         __recoveryMemo.set(history, { v, at: Date.now() });
014390:     return v;
014391: }
014392: // --- learned recovery ------------------------------------------------------------------------
014393: // The clock model (30 + sets·2.3h) is a population average; actual recovery is individual and muscle-
014394: // specific. This MEASURES it from the lifter's own history: performance-on-repeat. Each time a muscle
014395: // was retrained, pair the rest gap (hours since it was last trained) with the performance ratio (this
014396: // session's best e1RM ÷ the previous session's). Retrain before you've recovered and performance dips;
014397: // once recovered it holds — so a least-squares line ratio = a + b·gap identifies the rest at which
014398: // performance returns to the prior level, i.e. the recovery time. Shrunk toward the clock model by
014399: // confidence, clamped to a sane [18,120]h band, memoized on (history,part).
014400: //
014401: // Deliberately one-directional and conservative: it will RAISE recovery time for a lifter who
014402: // demonstrably dips on short rest (b>0, intercept<1), which is the case that matters — it protects a
014403: // slow-recovering muscle from being retrained (or trimmed) too eagerly. It stays silent (returns null →
014404: // clock model) when rest doesn't measurably help (b≈0), because "always fine at every gap I've tried"
014405: // cannot honestly prove a FASTER-than-clock recovery — only the absence of a dip, which isn't the same
014406: // thing. Identifiability floor: ≥8 repeat observations and ≥24h of gap spread, or it returns null.
014407: const __recovFitMemo = new WeakMap();

014580:         const cur = arr.filter(x => x.date > now - wk).map(x => x.best);

015114: /* ---- logged per-muscle volume: ONE window walk, ONE definition of "a set" ----------------------
015115:  *
015116:  * There used to be two of these. `weeklyActualVolume` and `loggedVolume` were the same function
015117:  * written 470 lines apart — same window, same direct-plus-fractional credit, differing only in a
015118:  * `<` versus `<=` and a parameterised `until` — and `volumeAdvice` called one of them while calling
015119:  * `underRecoveredWeekly` (which counts the other way) in the very next line. There is now one.
015120:  *
015121:  * Both of them counted `p.sets.length` raw. Since v493 a logged row is not necessarily a working
015122:  * set: myo-rep minis and drop sets are persisted with `sub: true` precisely so they can be told
015123:  * apart, and `isWorkSet` is the rule that tells them apart. Measured, three myo minis on an overhead
015124:  * press took logged shoulders from 3 to 6 and front delts from 3 to 6 — a doubling, on the card
015125:  * captioned "sets you've actually logged", and then read against MEV/MRV landmarks that are defined
015126:  * in working sets. An extension is stimulus, but it is not a set in the sense the landmark means,
015127:  * and the planned side (weeklyVolume) has never counted it as one. Warm-ups are already excluded
015128:  * upstream, at the point history is written; subs were not, and this is where that shows.
015129:  *
015130:  * The trailing-window walk is shared so the two variants cannot drift apart again: `logWindow` finds
015131:  * the working sets per exercise, and each variant only decides where to put the credit. */
015132: function logWindow(history, days, until, visit) {
015133:     const since = until - days * 86400000;
015134:     (history || []).forEach(h => {
015135:         if (!h || h.date <= since || h.date > until)
015136:             return;
015137:         Object.entries(h.perf || {}).forEach(([id, p]) => {
015138:             const ex = EX_BY_ID[id];

016056: function nextTMEvidence(program, history, id) {
016057:     const last = (history || []).find(h => h.perf?.[id] &&

016087: function summarizeSets(sets, ex, unit) {
016088:     /* SUB-SETS ARE NOT WORKING SETS. Drop sets and myo mini-sets are extensions performed at or below
016089:        the working load, deliberately short — a 4-rep mini or a stripped-load rep-out. Folding them in
016090:        corrupted the summary in both branches: at a shared load the straight-set branch takes the MIN
016091:        reps at the top weight, so one 4-rep mini reported the whole lift as 4 reps; at mixed loads the
016092:        ramp branch averaged the stripped weight in and under-prescribed. The summary is what next
016093:        session's opening load is built from, so it must describe the work, not the extensions. */
016094:     const done = sets.filter(s => s.done && !s.warm && !s.sub && parseInt(s.reps) > 0);
016095:     if (!done.length)
016096:         return null;
016097:     const wv = s => { const w = parseFloat(s.weight); return isNaN(w) ? 0 : w; };
016098:     // If any working set carried real load it's a loaded lift — summarise from the loaded sets only.
016099:     // Otherwise it's bodyweight (all 0/blank) or assisted (negative = machine/band help): keep those
016100:     // sets, and pick the "best" as the one closest to bodyweight — heaviest load, or LEAST assistance.
016101:     const hasPos = done.some(s => parseFloat(s.weight) > 0);
016102:     const pool = hasPos ? done.filter(s => parseFloat(s.weight) > 0) : done;
016103:     const weights = pool.map(wv);
016104:     const maxW = Math.max(...weights);
016105:     // Straight sets (every working set at the same load): summarise from the top tier exactly as before.
016106:     if (weights.every(w => w === weights[0])) {
016107:         const atTop = pool.filter(s => wv(s) === maxW);
016108:         const reps = Math.min(...atTop.map(s => parseInt(s.reps)));
016109:         return { weight: maxW, reps };
016110:     }
016111:     // Ramped / varying loads (e.g. 125→135→145→155): a single heaviest set isn't a load you actually
016112:     // held across the session, so anchoring next session on it over-prescribes. Summarise from the
016113:     // AVERAGE working load and the average reps instead — a weight you can realistically repeat across

016481: function plateauSessions(history, exId, dayId, limit = 6) {
016482:     const withEx = (history || []).filter(h => h.perf?.[exId]?.weight > 0);
016483:     if (!dayId)
016484:         return withEx.slice(0, limit);
016485:     const sameDay = withEx.filter(h => h.dayId === dayId);
016486:     // If this lift has only ever been trained on one day, scoping changed nothing — use everything, so
016487:     // a program whose dayIds were regenerated doesn't silently lose its history.
016488:     const days = new Set(withEx.map(h => h.dayId).filter(Boolean));
016489:     return (days.size <= 1 ? withEx : sameDay).slice(0, limit);
016490: }

006379: // The next session's position within a program, from its history: which day is due and which week.
006380: // dayIndex is the day AFTER the most recent logged session FOR THIS PROGRAM (schedule order when a
006381: // schedule is set, otherwise program-day order), wrapping at the end of the rotation; weekIndex
006382: // advances once per full rotation and wraps (including the deload week) at the program length. A
006383: // program with no history of its own starts at day 1, week 1 — so a freshly activated program does
006384: // NOT inherit the day cursor of whatever you trained last. Pure, so the cursor is unit-testable.
006385: // Continuous ("endless") mode: has a lift's estimated 1RM stopped moving? Looks at the best e1RM per
006386: // rotation across the program's main lifts over the last few rotations; a stall (no new best) is the
006387: // cue to pull a recovery deload forward instead of waiting for the fixed cadence. Pure + conservative
006388: // (only consulted once a few accumulation weeks are in), so it nudges rather than thrashes.
006389: function endlessStalled(program, entries, dpw) {
006390:     const mains = new Set((program.days || []).map(d => d.exercises?.[d.primaryIndex]).filter(Boolean));
006391:     if (!mains.size)
006392:         return false;
006393:     const rots = [[], [], []];
006394:     entries.forEach((h, i) => { const r = Math.floor(i / dpw); if (r < 3)
006395:         rots[r].push(h); });
006396:     const bestOf = (rot) => {
006397:         let best = 0;
006398:         rot.forEach(h => Object.entries(h.perf || {}).forEach(([id, p]) => {
006399:             if (!mains.has(id) || !p.sets)
006400:                 return;
006401:             p.sets.forEach(s => { if (s.w > 0 && s.r > 0)
006402:                 best = Math.max(best, e1rm(s.w, s.r)); });
006403:         }));
006404:         return best;
006405:     };
006406:     const [r0, r1, r2] = [bestOf(rots[0]), bestOf(rots[1]), bestOf(rots[2])];
006407:     if (!(r0 > 0 && r1 > 0 && r2 > 0))
006408:         return false; // need 3 full rotations of data
006409:     return r0 <= Math.max(r1, r2) + 1e-6; // newest rotation set no new best → stalled
006410: }

014372:  * training history to produce the identical answer. Measured at ~8ms per pass on a 200-session

014392: // --- learned recovery ------------------------------------------------------------------------
014393: // The clock model (30 + sets·2.3h) is a population average; actual recovery is individual and muscle-
014394: // specific. This MEASURES it from the lifter's own history: performance-on-repeat. Each time a muscle
014395: // was retrained, pair the rest gap (hours since it was last trained) with the performance ratio (this
014396: // session's best e1RM ÷ the previous session's). Retrain before you've recovered and performance dips;
014397: // once recovered it holds — so a least-squares line ratio = a + b·gap identifies the rest at which
014398: // performance returns to the prior level, i.e. the recovery time. Shrunk toward the clock model by
014399: // confidence, clamped to a sane [18,120]h band, memoized on (history,part).
014400: //
014401: // Deliberately one-directional and conservative: it will RAISE recovery time for a lifter who
014402: // demonstrably dips on short rest (b>0, intercept<1), which is the case that matters — it protects a
014403: // slow-recovering muscle from being retrained (or trimmed) too eagerly. It stays silent (returns null →
014404: // clock model) when rest doesn't measurably help (b≈0), because "always fine at every gap I've tried"
014405: // cannot honestly prove a FASTER-than-clock recovery — only the absence of a dip, which isn't the same
014406: // thing. Identifiability floor: ≥8 repeat observations and ≥24h of gap spread, or it returns null.
014407: const __recovFitMemo = new WeakMap();

018010:  *                resurrects it deliberately, which is what the edit meant.

018013:  * Property-tested to that effect, because a merge bug is not a visual glitch — it is someone's year.
018014:  */
018015: function mergeStores(local, incoming) {
018016:     if (!incoming || typeof incoming !== "object")
018017:         return { data: local, stats: null };
018018:     if (!local || typeof local !== "object")
018019:         return { data: incoming, stats: null };
018020:     const A = migrateStore(local), B = migrateStore(incoming);
018021:     const aNewer = (A.savedAt || 0) >= (B.savedAt || 0);
018022:     const older = aNewer ? B : A, newer = aNewer ? A : B;
018023:     const tombs = { ...(A.tombs || {}) };
018024:     Object.entries(B.tombs || {}).forEach(([id, t]) => { if (!(tombs[id] >= t))
018025:         tombs[id] = t; });

018027:     const byId = (a = [], b = [], count) => {
018028:         const m = new Map();
018029:         [...(Array.isArray(a) ? a : []), ...(Array.isArray(b) ? b : [])].forEach(r => {
018030:             if (!r || r.id == null)
018031:                 return;
018032:             const prev = m.get(r.id);
018033:             if (!prev) {
018034:                 m.set(r.id, r);
018035:                 return;
018036:             }
018037:             if (prev === r)
018038:                 return;
018039:             const pu = prev.updatedAt || 0, ru = r.updatedAt || 0;
018040:             if (ru > pu) {

018043:             }

030863: /* LONGHAND BORDERS ON PURPOSE. These style objects are SPREAD into call sites that then override a
030864:    single border part — `{ ...iconBtn(), borderColor: C.accent }` is the common shape, and the
030865:    focused-mode toggle overrides it CONDITIONALLY. Mixing a `border` shorthand with a longhand that
030866:    changes between renders makes React warn that the result can be wrong, and it genuinely can: which
030867:    one wins depends on property order. Emitting the three longhands keeps every override well-defined.
030868:    Do not "tidy" these back into a `border:` shorthand. */
030869: const iconBtn = () => ({ width: 38, height: 38, borderRadius: 12, borderWidth: 1, borderStyle: "solid", borderColor: C.border, background: C.card, color: C.text, display: "flex", alignItems: "center", justifyContent: "center", cursor: "pointer", flexShrink: 0 });

032323:     const stats = useMemo(() => {
032324:         const now = Date.now();
032325:         const thisWeek = history.filter(h => h && h.date > now - 7 * 86400000).length;
032326:         // Week streak: consecutive 7-day windows (ending today) that contain at least one workout.
032327:         // This rewards consistency without punishing the rest days a good program builds in — a
032328:         // 3x/week lifter keeps their streak, where a raw consecutive-DAY streak would reset constantly.
032329:         const has = (a, b) => history.some(h => h.date > now - b * 86400000 && h.date <= now - a * 86400000);
032330:         let weekStreak = 0;
032331:         for (let w = 0; w < 200; w++) {
032332:             if (has(w * 7, (w + 1) * 7))
032333:                 weekStreak++;
032334:             else
032335:                 break;
032336:         }
032337:         return { total: history.length, thisWeek, weekStreak };

032922: // The app shell (index.html, app.js, app.css) is served cache-first by the service worker for
032923: // instant loads and full offline support — which also means a shipped code fix can sit on the
032924: // server indefinitely without ever reaching an already-visited device until the normal
032925: // detect-new-worker → "Restart" flow completes. If that flow itself never gets a chance to run (the
032926: // tab stays open, the update check hasn't fired yet, or an EARLIER cached version had a bug in the
032927: // update-checker itself), there was previously no way out of a stale build short of digging through
032928: // the browser's OS-level site-data settings — which also wipes localStorage, taking workout history
032929: // and saved programs down with it. This is the safe, in-app escape hatch: it clears only the
032930: // service worker's registration and its CacheStorage entries (a completely separate storage system
032931: // from localStorage), then reloads — the freshly-registered worker re-fetches everything from the
032932: // network, guaranteeing the latest deployed build. History, programs, and settings are untouched.
032933: function ForceRefreshRow() {
032934:     const [busy, setBusy] = useState(false);
032935:     const supported = typeof navigator !== "undefined" && "serviceWorker" in navigator && typeof caches !== "undefined";
032936:     if (!supported)
032937:         return null;
032938:     const doRefresh = async () => {
032939:         setBusy(true);
032940:         try {
032941:             const regs = await navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistrations();
032942:             await Promise.all(regs.map(r => r.unregister()));
032943:             const keys = await caches.keys();
032944:             await Promise.all(keys.map(k => caches.delete(k)));
032945:         }
032946:         catch { }
032947:         location.reload();
032948:     };
--- hit 13 lines 33689-33742 ---

033690:             return a.part === b.part ? (a.pri - b.pri) : PART_ORDER.indexOf(a.part) - PART_ORDER.indexOf(b.part);
033691:         });
033692:     }, [query, part, equip, flag, goals, banned, recentSeen]);

002590: /* Every id that behaves like a selectorized machine — supported path, pin stack, cheap to set up and

003264: // assistance). Progress = less assistance (toward 0 → bodyweight → added load), so their

006572:         // forever: load and volume autoregulate every session, and a light deload rotation is inserted
006573:         // automatically when accumulation has run its course (a soft cap) OR a main lift stalls, whichever
006574:         // comes first. Deloads are detected straight from history (a session logged at the deload sentinel
006575:         // week, weekIndex > cap), so the cadence self-corrects even when a stall pulls one in early.
006576:         const cap = weeksOf(program); // accumulation weeks before an auto-deload
006577:         const isDeloadEntry = (h) => h.weekIndex != null && h.weekIndex > cap;
006578:         let headDeloads = 0;
006579:         for (const h of entries) {
006580:             if (isDeloadEntry(h))
006581:                 headDeloads++;
006582:             else
006583:                 break;
006584:         }
006585:         // Mid deload rotation → keep the rest of the rotation light until every day has been deloaded.
006586:         if (headDeloads > 0 && headDeloads < dpw) {
006587:             return { dayIndex, weekIndex: cap + 1, done, scheduled, lastIdx, endless: true, accumWk: 0, deload: true };
006588:         }
006589:         // Otherwise count the accumulation sessions since the last deload and place us in the wave.
006590:         let sinceDeload = 0;
006591:         for (const h of entries) {
006592:             if (isDeloadEntry(h))
006593:                 break;
006594:             sinceDeload++;
006595:         }
006596:         const accumWk = Math.floor(sinceDeload / dpw) + 1;
006597:         const stalled = accumWk >= 3 && endlessStalled(program, entries, dpw);
006598:         const deloadDue = accumWk > cap || stalled;
006599:         const weekIndex = deloadDue ? cap + 1 : Math.min(accumWk, cap);
006600:         return { dayIndex, weekIndex, done, scheduled, lastIdx, endless: true, accumWk: deloadDue ? 0 : accumWk, deload: deloadDue, stalled };
006601:     }

006605:        history entry, or logging the same planned day twice counted as two steps through the block. That
006606:        can jump Program View into week 2/3/a deload even though a day in the current week was never
006607:        completed; since later weeks can intentionally carry fewer sets, it looks exactly like the program

012290: function repRange(goal, ex, isPrimary, phase) {
012291:     const [lo, hi] = ex.rep;
012292:     const strength = goal === "strength" || (goal === "both" && isPrimary);
012293:     if (!strength)
012294:         return [lo, hi];
012295:     // Isolation (single-joint) lifts don't get loaded into low-rep strength territory even in a
012296:     // strength block: heavy 3–6 rep curls, flyes, or lateral raises are awkward, joint-stressful,
012297:     // and build no meaningful strength the way compounds do — their job is hypertrophy volume. Keep
012298:     // them in their natural range (trimming only the very top rep so they're not pure burnout sets).
012299:     // Strength periodization belongs on the compounds, where it actually drives force production.
012300:     if (ex.type === "isolation") {
012301:         const iHi = Math.max(lo + 2, hi - 2);
012302:         return [lo, Math.max(lo + 1, iHi)];
012303:     }
012304:     const sLo = clamp(Math.round(lo * 0.56), 2, Math.max(2, lo - 1));
012305:     const sHi = clamp(Math.round(hi * 0.6), sLo + 1, hi);
012306:     if (phase == null)
012307:         return [sLo, sHi];
012308:     if (phase < 0.4) { // accumulation — volume base at moderate loads
012309:         const aLo = Math.round((sLo + lo) / 2);
012310:         const aHi = Math.max(aLo + 1, Math.round((sHi + hi) / 2));
012311:         return [aLo, aHi];
012312:     }
012313:     if (phase < 0.8)
012314:         return [sLo, sHi]; // intensification — the classic strength zone
012315:     const rLo = Math.max(2, sLo - 1); // realization / peak — heavy doubles-to-triples territory
012316:     const rHi = Math.max(rLo + 1, sHi - 2);
012317:     return [rLo, rHi];
012318: }

012320:    ~3 RIR in week 1 down toward failure by week 4, then a high-RIR deload.

015592:    extension) and it belongs here on the same reasoning as hamstrings and glutes. */
015593: const LOWER_PARTS = ["quads", "hamstrings", "glutes"];
015594: /* ONE OWNER for "does this lift take the big linear jump" — and it is a question about the MOVEMENT,
015595:  * not about the part label.
015596:  *
015597:  * ⚠ THE BUG THIS FIXES. `deadlift` has part `lower_back`, which LOWER_PARTS omits, so every deadlift
015598:  * variant took the UPPER-body increment: measured, a 5/3/1 training max went 205 → 210 (+5) while the
015599:  * squat beside it went 245 → 255 (+10). Wendler is explicit that squat and deadlift both advance +10
015600:  * lb a cycle and bench and press +5, and the deadlift is a named main lift of FIVE percent-scheme
015601:  * programs here — five_three_one, five31_beginner, gzclp, rippler, jt. It was under-progressing in all
015602:  * of them. Found by coverage: `projectNextTM` and `gzAdvance` had never executed once, in the engine
015603:  * sweep or in any of the 97 gates.
015604:  *
015605:  * ⚠ AND WHY THE OBVIOUS FIX IS WRONG. Adding "lower_back" to LOWER_PARTS looks equivalent and is not:
015606:  * that part also files `superman` (bodyweight, no load at all) and `back-ext-45`, and both would
015607:  * inherit a 10 lb jump identical to a back squat's. Measured on that version — superman linearInc 10.
015608:  * `movePattern` alone does not separate them either, because `back-ext-45` is also a hinge. The test
015609:  * that does is hinge AND externally bar-loaded, which is true of all five deadlift variants and false
015610:  * for both accessories.
015611:  *
015612:  * LOADSTEP IS DELIBERATELY LEFT ON LOWER_PARTS. That site asks a different question — the smallest
015613:  * jump the HARDWARE can make — and answering it with a progression rule would move loads for every
015614:  * lifter with a lower_back lift, not just the ones on a percent scheme. Different question, different
015615:  * owner, and the separation is pinned by the gate. */
015616: function lowerBodyLift(ex) {
015617:     if (!ex)
015618:         return false;
015619:     if (LOWER_PARTS.includes(ex.part))
015620:         return true;
015621:     return ex.part === "lower_back" && movePattern(ex) === "hinge" && isBarLike(ex.equip);
015622: }

015624: // Stored with the unit it was set in, so a unit switch safely falls back to defaults.

015632: function loadStep(ex, unit) {
015633:     // A per-exercise increment (e.g. a specific machine's pin-stack notch) wins over everything —
015634:     // it's the user telling us exactly how this machine actually moves.
015635:     if (ex && EX_LOAD_INC[ex.id] && EX_LOAD_INC[ex.id].v > 0 && EX_LOAD_INC[ex.id].unit === unit)
015636:         return EX_LOAD_INC[ex.id].v;
015637:     // User-set global plate increment wins next when it applies (their actual smallest jump).
015638:     if (LOAD_INC.v > 0 && LOAD_INC.unit === unit)
015639:         return LOAD_INC.v;
015640:     const lower = LOWER_PARTS.includes(ex.part);
015641:     // Fixed/selectorized loads jump coarser than a micro-loadable barbell: a dumbbell pair or a
015642:     // machine stack typically moves in ~5 lb / 2.5 kg notches per hand or plate, and you can't
015643:     // split it. Reflect that so progression math doesn't assume bar-fine increments it can't make.
015644:     const eq = ex.equip || [];
015645:     const fixedJump = (eq.includes("dumbbell") || eq.includes("kettlebell")) && !isBarLike(eq);
015646:     const machineJump = isMachineLike(eq) && !isBarLike(eq) && !eq.includes("dumbbell");
015647:     // Isolations on a machine/cable (triceps pushdown, lateral raise, curl, leg extension) should not
015648:     // jump a full pin-stack notch — 10 lb on a small muscle is a 15-30% leap that stalls progress.
015649:     // Treat compound machine work (leg press, pulldown, row, hack squat) as the coarse 10 lb stack,
015650:     // but step isolations at 5 lb (and cables, which usually have finer increments, at 5 lb too).
015651:     // Only a true selectorized/plate machine stack (leg press, hack squat, plate-loaded row) jumps
015652:     // coarse and can't be split. Cables usually have add-on weights or finer pins, and any barbell
015653:     // takes a pair of 2.5 lb plates — so all barbell & freeweight work, all cables, and machine
015654:     // ISOLATIONS step the fine 5 lb / 2.5 kg. That keeps progression sustainable (a 10 lb jump on a
015655:     // squat or a pulldown is a big leap most weeks); the coarse 10 lb / 5 kg is reserved for compound
015656:     // machine stacks where it's genuinely the smallest notch (and any machine can be set exactly via
015657:     // its per-exercise increment).
015658:     const eqCable = eq.includes("cable") && !isBarLike(eq) && !eq.includes("dumbbell");
015659:     const coarseMachine = machineJump && !eqCable && ex.type !== "isolation"; // compound plate/pin stack
015660:     if (unit === "lb") {
015661:         if (fixedJump)
015662:             return lower ? 10 : 5; // DB/KB pairs move in ~5 lb-per-hand notches
015663:         if (coarseMachine)
015664:             return 10; // leg press / hack squat / plate-loaded stack
015665:         return 5; // barbell, freeweight, cables, machine isolations
015666:     }
015667:     if (fixedJump)
015668:         return lower ? 4 : 2;
015669:     if (coarseMachine)
015670:         return 5;
015671:     return 2.5;
015672: }

016076:             return;
016077:         }
016078:         const lower = lowerBodyLift(ex);
016079:         // Floor the cycle bump at this lift's smallest real step so a custom coarse increment (e.g. a
016080:         // machine-based main lift) doesn't get rounded back to the current TM, stalling progression.
016081:         const inc = Math.max(unit === "lb" ? (lower ? 10 : 5) : (lower ? 5 : 2.5), loadStep(ex, unit));
016082:         const { factor } = nextTMEvidence(program, history, id);
016083:         out[id] = factor > 0 ? roundTo(tm[id] + inc * factor, loadStep(ex, unit)) : tm[id];
016084:     });
016085:     return out;
016086: }
016087: function summarizeSets(sets, ex, unit) {
016088:     /* SUB-SETS ARE NOT WORKING SETS. Drop sets and myo mini-sets are extensions performed at or below
016089:        the working load, deliberately short — a 4-rep mini or a stripped-load rep-out. Folding them in
016090:        corrupted the summary in both branches: at a shared load the straight-set branch takes the MIN
016091:        reps at the top weight, so one 4-rep mini reported the whole lift as 4 reps; at mixed loads the
016092:        ramp branch averaged the stripped weight in and under-prescribed. The summary is what next
016093:        session's opening load is built from, so it must describe the work, not the extensions. */
016094:     const done = sets.filter(s => s.done && !s.warm && !s.sub && parseInt(s.reps) > 0);
016095:     if (!done.length)
016096:         return null;
016097:     const wv = s => { const w = parseFloat(s.weight); return isNaN(w) ? 0 : w; };
016098:     // If any working set carried real load it's a loaded lift — summarise from the loaded sets only.
016099:     // Otherwise it's bodyweight (all 0/blank) or assisted (negative = machine/band help): keep those
016100:     // sets, and pick the "best" as the one closest to bodyweight — heaviest load, or LEAST assistance.
016101:     const hasPos = done.some(s => parseFloat(s.weight) > 0);
016102:     const pool = hasPos ? done.filter(s => parseFloat(s.weight) > 0) : done;
016103:     const weights = pool.map(wv);
016104:     const maxW = Math.max(...weights);
016105:     // Straight sets (every working set at the same load): summarise from the top tier exactly as before.
016106:     if (weights.every(w => w === weights[0])) {
016107:         const atTop = pool.filter(s => wv(s) === maxW);
016108:         const reps = Math.min(...atTop.map(s => parseInt(s.reps)));
016109:         return { weight: maxW, reps };
016110:     }
016111:     // Ramped / varying loads (e.g. 125→135→145→155): a single heaviest set isn't a load you actually
016112:     // held across the session, so anchoring next session on it over-prescribes. Summarise from the
016113:     // AVERAGE working load and the average reps instead — a weight you can realistically repeat across
016114:     // all sets. (Per-set e1RM and PRs still come from the raw `sets` array, so a top-set PR isn't lost.)
016115:     // Round the average onto the exercise's REAL increment (pin-stack notch / plate pair), not a flat
016116:     // 0.5 — a raw mean like 36.5 on a 5 lb machine is a weight that doesn't exist, and it leaked into
016117:     // "hold the same load" suggestions and the "Last" display as an impossible setting.
016118:     const stepW = ex ? loadStep(ex, unit) : 0.5;
016119:     const avgW = roundTo(weights.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / weights.length, stepW);
016120:     const reps = Math.round(pool.reduce((a, s) => a + parseInt(s.reps), 0) / pool.length);
016121:     return { weight: avgW, reps };
016122: }
016123: /* Dynamic warm-up & mobility, tailored to the day's movements (raise temp → mobilize the
016124:    joints you're about to load → a couple of ramp sets, which the logger already programs). */

016126:     quads: ["Bodyweight squats × 15", "Walking lunges × 10/side", "Ankle rocks × 10/side"],
016127:     hamstrings: ["Leg swings front-to-back × 10/side", "Bodyweight hip hinges × 12"],
016128:     glutes: ["Glute bridges × 15", "Lateral band walks × 10/side"],
016129:     upper_back: ["Cat–cow × 8", "Band pull-aparts × 15", "Scapular pull-ups / dead hang × 20s"],

016136:     core: ["Dead bug × 8/side", "Cat–cow × 8"],

016138:     traps: ["Shrug rolls × 10", "Band pull-aparts × 15"],
016139:     forearms: ["Wrist circles × 10", "Wrist flexor/extensor stretch × 20s"]
016140: };
016141: function warmupRoutine(day) {
016142:     if (!day || !day.exercises || !day.exercises.length)
016143:         return null;
016144:     const order = [day.primaryIndex, ...day.exercises.map((_, i) => i).filter(i => i !== day.primaryIndex)];
016145:     const parts = [];
016146:     order.forEach(i => { const ex = EX_BY_ID[day.exercises[i]]; if (ex && !parts.includes(ex.part))
016147:         parts.push(ex.part); });
016148:     const moves = ["3–5 min easy cardio to raise your core temperature"];
016149:     parts.slice(0, 3).forEach(p => (MOBILITY[p] || []).forEach(m => { if (!moves.includes(m))
016150:         moves.push(m); }));
016151:     const out = moves.slice(0, 6);
016152:     out.push("Then ramp with 1–2 light sets on your first lift before working weight");
016153:     return out;
016154: }
016155: /* warm-up ramp before the first working set.
016156:    The number of warm-up sets a lift warrants scales with how big & heavy it is (mirroring the
016157:    per-exercise warm-up counts in evidence-based hypertrophy programs): a primary loaded compound
016158:    gets the full pyramid, an accessory compound a shorter ramp, an isolation a single feeler set. */
016159: function warmupCount(ex, isPrimary, workingWeight = 0, unit = "kg") {
016160:     const comp = ex.type === "compound";
016161:     const heavy = ex.rep[0] <= 6; // low-rep, heavily loaded
016162:     let n = (isPrimary && comp) ? (heavy ? 3 : 2) : comp ? (heavy ? 2 : 1) : 1;
016163:     /* ---- Depth must follow the LOAD, not just the slot ------------------------------------------
016164:      *
016165:      * The rules above key off the exercise's default rep range and whether it happens to be the day's
016166:      * primary. Neither is the thing that makes a warm-up necessary. A back squat sitting in slot two
016167:      * scored 2 warm-ups no matter the weight, so at 495 lb the ramp ran 250 → 345 → 495: a 150 lb,
016168:      * 43% jump straight into the top set, on the most axially loaded movement there is. The same lift
016169:      * at 135 lb got the identical ramp, which is over-warming a light day and under-warming a heavy one
016170:      * with one rule.
016171:      *
016172:      * So scale with how far above the empty bar the working set actually is. A bar-plus-a-plate squat
016173:      * needs one or two feeler sets; a triple-bodyweight squat needs to be approached. Ratios are to the
016174:      * bar because that is the floor you must pass through anyway, and they only ever ADD steps — this
016175:      * can shorten nothing, so no light day gets a longer ramp than before. Capped at 4 by warmupPlan;
016176:      * useless steps are pruned downstream by warmupSets. */
016177:     if (comp && workingWeight > 0) {
016178:         const bar = BARS[barFor(ex)]?.[unit] || 0;
016179:         if (bar > 0) {
016180:             const mult = workingWeight / bar;
016181:             if (mult >= 3)
016182:                 n = Math.max(n, 3); // ~135+ on a 45lb bar: needs a real ramp
016183:             if (mult >= 5)
016184:                 n = Math.max(n, 4); // ~225+: approach it in four
016185:         }
016186:         else {
016187:             /* Machines and cables have no bar to measure against, so use the lift's own load step as the
016188:                yardstick — 20+ increments above nothing is a heavy machine press however it is loaded. */
--- hit 24 lines 16139-16192 ---
016139:     forearms: ["Wrist circles × 10", "Wrist flexor/extensor stretch × 20s"]
016140: };

016173:      * needs one or two feeler sets; a triple-bodyweight squat needs to be approached. Ratios are to the
016174:      * bar because that is the floor you must pass through anyway, and they only ever ADD steps — this
016175:      * can shorten nothing, so no light day gets a longer ramp than before. Capped at 4 by warmupPlan;
